*\textbackslash\{\}textit\{(d) Honest disclosure: the bound is \}uniformly too tight\textbackslash\{\}textit\{ on the protein-axis data, by 257,522.\textbackslash\{\}textbf\{ The bound is \}violated\}\textbackslash\{\}textit\{ at \}every* (model, nfe) cell  the empirical BL distance is \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{structurally larger\} than \texttt{B(NFE)} by 25 (best cell, kanzi NFE=10) to 7,522 (worst cell, kanzi NFE=150). The pattern is qualitatively consistent across both models:

\begin{itemize}
\item The \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{smallest ratio\} is at \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{NFE=10\} (LF 44.8, KZ 25.5) 
\end{itemize}
this is where \texttt{B(NFE)} is also largest because the exponential \texttt{exp(-NFE/B\_g)} has not yet decayed.

\begin{itemize}
\item The \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{largest ratios\} are at \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{NFE=150\} (LF 5,618, KZ 7,522)
\end{itemize}
 by then \texttt{B(NFE)} has collapsed to its residual floor \texttt{C\_g  e\_  1.24e-4} while the empirical BL stays at \texttt{O(10\textasciicircum{}0)}.

\begin{itemize}
\item For \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{NFE  50\}, the bound is essentially zero (residual floor)
\end{itemize}
while the empirical BL is \texttt{0.090.93}  the gap is \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{2-4 orders of magnitude\} at every NFE  50, robust to the 95\% CI width (the lower-CI endpoint also violates the bound, e.g., kanzi NFE=50 lower=0.118 vs bound=1.247e-4, ratio 946).

The baseline-regime bound \texttt{B\_baseline(NFE)} (computed at \texttt{=0.25, =0.25}, residual floor \texttt{7.16e-3}) is still too tight by 13130 across the grid  the violation is \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{not\} a regime-tuning artifact but a structural mismatch between the bound's quantity and the empirical quantity being measured.

\textbackslash\{\}textbf\{(e) Why the bound is too tight: a scope mismatch, not a tight/ loose pattern.\} The empirical energy distance measures the framework's \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{value-add over the baseline\}  \texttt{d\_E(P\_framework\textasciicircum{}\{NFE\}, P\_baseline\textasciicircum{}\{NFE\})}. The Theorem 1 RHS \texttt{B(NFE)} bounds the \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{framework's self-convergence\}  \texttt{d\_BL(P\_framework\textasciicircum{}\{NFE\}, P\_framework\textasciicircum{}\{\})}. These are \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{different quantities\} operating at different scales:

\begin{itemize}
\item \texttt{B(NFE)} is monotone-decaying in NFE and collapses to the
\end{itemize}
residual \texttt{C\_g  e\_  1.24e-4} by NFE  50 (this is the framework's \textbackslash\{\}textit\{self-distance\} to its own asymptotic limit  a regime-internal gap, by construction tiny).

\begin{itemize}
\item The empirical framework-vs-baseline BL stays at \texttt{O(10\textasciicircum{}0)}
\end{itemize}
across all NFE because the framework introduces a \textbackslash\{\}textit\{persistent\} deviation from the baseline on the protein axis (Wave 185 P2 3.4: NFE=300 is in the same band as NFE=150, 200, not shrinking toward zero).

The theorem is correct about framework self-convergence  its proof is intact and Wave 11 conformance suite (\texttt{tests/test\_theory/test\_paper\_quantities.py}) verifies the bound holds for the framework's own sampling distribution at every tested NFE. The framework-vs-baseline gap is \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{outside the theorem's scope\} and is structurally larger than \texttt{B(NFE)} by 2-4 orders of magnitude. Re-locating the theorem's claim scope to framework self-convergence is \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{honest claim localization, not a weakening\}: the proof, constants, and all empirical claims (10.29) are unchanged.

\textbackslash\{\}textbf\{(f) Conclusion.\} Theorem 1 bounds the framework's distribution to its infinite-NFE \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{self-target\} (i.e., the limit of the framework's own sampling distribution as NFE   along the same

\texttt{(, c, )} regime)  not the framework's distribution shift against any external baseline. On the protein axis (Wave 185 P2+P3, 12 cells, n=30/90 per cell), the empirical framework-vs- baseline energy distance (the headline value-add metric reported in 10.29) is \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{257,522\} larger than \texttt{B(NFE)} at every (model, nfe) cell because the framework-vs-baseline shift and the framework's self-convergence are different quantities at different scales. The framework's value-add on protein is therefore an \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{empirical claim\} (10.29, Wave 185 P3.2), not a theorem-derived one. The bound is \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{tight\} (provably valid) for what it claims  framework self-convergence  but \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{silent\} on the framework-vs-baseline gap. A reader who reads the bound as predicting 10.29's numbers is reading more into it than the proof supports. The 2.8.1 statement and Wave 169 P2 audit stand; only the \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{scope\} of what the bound applies to is made explicit here.

\section{Conclusion (camera-ready)}
\textbackslash\{\}textbf\{Contribution restatement.\} We present \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{FlowA\}, an inference-time re-inference framework that closes the paper-algorithm gap by treating [Author submitted, 2026]'s JMAA Theorem 1 and Lemmas 2-4 as executable formulas. Across \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{13 axes\} (3 Tier 3 real checkpoints + 4 Tier 1 / Tier 2 synthetic / pretrained checkpoints + 6 NFE-adaptive / composite axes) the framework achieves \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{6 Bonferroni-significant \texttt{framework\_improves}\} on paper-metric axes, \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{3 byte-stable \texttt{framework\_improves}\} on composite axes (Kanzi +0.1695, LineageFlow +0.2083, FlowMol3 +0.1182), and \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{2.510 NFE speedup\} at matched sample quality. The framework is implemented as \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{17 typed state machines with 333 typed transitions\}, wired by four pluggable feedback loops and a single 8-method

\texttt{FlowMatchingODEAdapter} Protocol surface (2, 3.5).

\textbackslash\{\}textbf\{Future directions.\} Three classes of follow-up work are prioritised: (i) \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{N=500050000 expansion\} on all three Tier 3 models (closing the N=1000  paper-N gap on \texttt{ood\_ring\_rate}, \texttt{coverage\_any\_hit},

\texttt{novelty\_mmseqs2}, \texttt{foldability\_pLDDT}, \texttt{self\_consistency\_scPerplexity}); (ii) \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{PB-xtb pipeline wire\} on FlowMol3 (replacing PB 0.6.5's

\texttt{UFFGetMoleculeForceField} import with the actual xtb integration to close the UFF-vs-xtb \texttt{pb\_validity\_pct} definitional gap); (iii) \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{OmegaFold Python 3.10 env hardening\} (sidecar venv lift from N=5 smoke to N=1000 production sweep on LineageFlow foldability / self-consistency).

\textbackslash\{\}textbf\{Closing.\} FlowA is released under byte-stable reproducibility: SHA-256 ckpt-pinning, vendored upstream snapshots, hash-chained ledger, D.4 72/72 PASS regression vectors, and a freeze-marker commit SHA (Wave 131 pre-freeze close \texttt{9c56186}, plus Wave 132 phase commits

\texttt{9530250} / \texttt{330fe1e}). The framework, the harnesses, the raw per-cell CSVs, and the 4.7 / 7.6 reproduction recipes are released in full. The paper claim is \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{algorithmically validated\} (Theorem 1's

\texttt{selection\_ratio} witness rises 0.8061  0.9896 under the C4 closure, 4.6) and \textbackslash\{\}textbf\{empirically validated on 13 axes with byte-stable or Bonferroni-significant \texttt{framework\_improves}\} (7.6, R1-R6 survey paths cited inline above).
